**Short answer: No — we have completed the core control-plane (roughly Phases 0–4, 7, 9, 10 partially, plus two unplanned repairs), but the majority of the operational/telemetry/qualification workstream (Phases 5–6, 8, 11–16) is still open.**

---

# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Implementation Plan v0.2.1

**Status:** Design decisions incorporated; electricity-cost composition corrected; ready for read-only infrastructure preflight before deployment scripting  
**Decision date:** 2026-09-08  
**Target Proxmox host:** `MIAM-00135`  
**Candidate VMID:** `108` — **must be proven unused before creation**  
**Candidate IP:** `10.0.20.108` — **must be proven unused before assignment**  
**DNS name:** `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`  
**Primary purpose:** Central control plane, OpenAI-compatible API gateway, Hermes-agent key authority, routing layer, benchmarking system, observability system, and cost/performance database for the MARION-IA-USA local LLM infrastructure.

---

## 1. Executive objective

Create a dedicated VM named:

```text
MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
```

on `MIAM-00135`, initially bootstrapped from the known-good GPU Driver Installer controller VM architecture but fully re-identified and stripped of copied GDI secrets/state.

The LLM Manager will become the stable point through which local Hermes agents and other authorized internal clients use LLM inference. It should:

- monitor and control vLLM deployments on `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00112`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- issue a distinct API key to each Hermes agent
- expose a stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint
- route requests to healthy local deployments first
- wait a deliberate, configurable interval for local capacity instead of prematurely buying cloud inference
- fall back to OpenRouter only when policy permits and local capacity/health criteria are not satisfied
- enforce OpenRouter spend caps and per-agent policy
- benchmark model/topology combinations and keep historical results
- collect GPU, vLLM, request, health, and power telemetry
- calculate energy cost and performance-per-dollar
- use GPU onboard telemetry as the primary dynamic local power signal
- ingest Emporia whole-circuit/PDU/mini-split data as an independent facility-level measurement source
- store operational metadata and history in PostgreSQL
- offload high-volume or full prompt/response history away from the constrained `MIAM-00135` system disk
- provide controlled vLLM service lifecycle actions and bounded VM recovery actions
- provide a future path for multi-node vLLM deployments using the 100 Gb interconnect between `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- eventually compare/rank local inference, OpenRouter, direct APIs, and other officially supported provider integrations by availability, quality, cost, performance, context, privacy, and policy

The LLM Manager is a **control plane and gateway**. It does not normally host model weights or perform GPU inference itself.

---

## 2. Decisions locked in v0.2

The following answers from Section 28 of v0.1 are now treated as design decisions.

| Item | v0.2 decision |
|---|---|
| VM identity | Candidate `VMID 108`, candidate `10.0.20.108`; fail closed unless both are proven unused |
| Gateway | LiteLLM Proxy plus a Startup Teams custom management/control web application |
| Client keys | One distinct API key per Hermes agent |
| Qwen 3.6 normal load | Design around about 10 concurrent agents/requests, with an initial hard concurrency envelope of about 20 pending benchmark qualification |
| Routing default | Local first, deliberate wait/queue, OpenRouter fallback only if the key policy allows it |
| Cloud policy | Default OpenRouter master/aggregate budget must have hard caps; individual keys may override fallback permissions within administrator limits |
| VM control | Manager may control both vLLM service lifecycle and bounded inference-VM recovery/restart |
| Power | GPU telemetry first; modeled CPU/RAM/platform overhead; Emporia used as independent whole-circuit/facility telemetry |
| Electricity planning rate | Rate 400 base energy (`$0.09992/kWh` Sep-May; `$0.13398/kWh` Jun-Aug) + year-round Emporia-observed variable fees/riders adder of `$0.05830/kWh`; effective planning rates `$0.15822/kWh` Sep-May and `$0.19228/kWh` Jun-Aug (displayed as 15.82¢ and 19.23¢) |
| Logging | Metadata-first initially; architecture must support full prompt/response logs later with off-host storage |
| Admin auth | Existing LDAP/LLDAP identity system; local break-glass/bootstrap admin only if needed |
| DNS | `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` |
| Database | PostgreSQL; application designed so PostgreSQL can reside on a future storage-oriented VM on `MIAM-00147` |
| Fallback timing | Local requests are given meaningful time to queue/run; slow local TTFT alone is not a reason to immediately duplicate the request in OpenRouter |
| Distributed inference | 100 Gb multi-node path is a first-class future capability but remains unroutable until independently qualified |

### Important interpretation: API keys versus concurrency

The number of issued API keys should **not** be limited to 20. API keys identify clients; concurrency consumes model capacity.

For the known `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` deployment, start with a deployment profile such as:

```yaml
recommended_concurrency: 10
initial_hard_concurrency_limit: 20
max_issued_agent_keys: unlimited_by_model_capacity
```

The concurrency values must be model/deployment specific. A different model, quantization, topology, context target, or GPU set may receive a different admission envelope.

---

## 3. Known inference baseline

### 3.1 MIAM-00111 qualified baseline

Known qualified environment:

```text
Host:             MIAM-00111
Proxmox:          VE 9.2.10
Inference VM:     VM103 jordatech-vllm-test
Guest:            Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS
Kernel:           6.8.0-139-generic
GPUs:             6 x RTX 3080 20 GB
Aggregate VRAM:   120 GB
NVIDIA driver:    R580 open, module 580.173.02
Python env:       /opt/vllm-venv
API port:         8000
Model:            QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
```

Working topology:

```text
TP1 / DP6 / EP6
```

Core flags:

```text
--tensor-parallel-size 1
--data-parallel-size 6
--enable-expert-parallel
--expert-placement-strategy linear
--quantization awq
--dtype half
--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92
--kv-cache-dtype turboquant_k8v4
--attention-backend TURBOQUANT
--enable-prefix-caching
--enable-chunked-prefill
--scheduler-reserve-full-isl
--watermark 0.05
--kv-cache-metrics
```

Validated large-context operating point:

```text
10 concurrent requests
229,376 input tokens per request
+ 256 output tokens per request
```

Do not record the synthetic 250k test as a qualified model limit because tokenizer expansion exceeded the declared 262,144-token model maximum.

Representative 4096-in/256-out throughput results:

| Concurrency | Output tok/s | Total tok/s | Mean TTFT |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 249.85 | 4259.49 | 4.27 s |
| 16 | 271.21 | 4623.65 | 4.73 s |
| 20 | 311.98 | 5318.69 | 5.20 s |
| 32 | 319.69 | 5450.14 | 7.39 s |
| 48 | 348.62 | 5943.41 | 11.13 s |

For normal Hermes service, concurrency 10 remains the initial preferred baseline even though aggregate throughput rises at higher concurrency.

### 3.2 MIAM-00112

Known major difference:

```text
6 x RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB
AMD Threadripper Pro 3955WX
128 GB RAM
```

Do not copy the MIAM-00111 topology without qualification. Its 96 GB aggregate VRAM and different GPU architecture require a separate model-fit and benchmark envelope.

### 3.3 MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144

These are intended to reproduce or complement the MIAM-00111 inference stack, but each must independently inventory:

```text
GPU model/count/VRAM
PCI BDFs
IOMMU groups
kernel
NVIDIA driver/module packages
inference VM resources
100 Gb NICs/ports
network addresses
RDMA capability
GPU-to-NIC PCIe topology
```

Never copy PCI BDFs between hosts.

```

## 5. Base VM and bootstrap strategy

### 5.1 Candidate VM sizing

Start with:

```text
VMID:             108 candidate only
IP:               10.0.20.108 candidate only
Hostname:         marion-ia-usa-llm-manager
Display name:     MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
OS:               Ubuntu Server 24.04 LTS
vCPU:             8
RAM:              16 GB
System disk:      64 GB
Local data disk:  64-128 GB only if actually available/needed
Machine:          q35
Firmware:         OVMF
QEMU agent:       enabled
Ballooning:       disabled
Autostart:        enabled after acceptance test
GPU passthrough:  none
```

Because `MIAM-00135` has constrained storage, do not assume a large local telemetry/database disk. Keep all high-volume paths configurable and relocatable.

### 5.2 Clone source

Use the existing controller:

```text
VM107
gpu-driver-installer-v4-1
10.0.20.107:8443
```

as a **bootstrap image only**.

Required clone hygiene:

1. Full-clone while source is in a consistent state.
2. Keep clone network-isolated until identity reset is complete.
3. Change VM display name and guest hostname.
4. Regenerate machine-id.
5. Regenerate SSH host keys.
6. Remove copied application secrets, private keys, audit records, state, tokens, and TLS identity.
7. Remove GDI application code/services not needed by LLM Manager.
8. Retain only useful base hardening, QEMU guest agent, service patterns, reverse-proxy/TLS patterns, and OS configuration.
9. Generate a new TLS certificate for `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`.
10. Run a secret scan before enabling the new network interface.

### 5.3 Fail-closed VMID/IP preflight

Before any clone or network mutation, the deployment script must prove both identifiers are unused.

VMID validation should include at least:

```text
cluster-wide Proxmox resource lookup for VMID 108
MIAM-00135 qm list
/etc/pve configuration search
```

IP validation should include at least:

```text
existing Proxmox guest configuration search
DNS lookup
neighbor/ARP table lookup
ICMP probe
ARP duplicate-address detection from the correct VLAN
DHCP/static reservation inspection where available
```

A failed probe does **not** prove an IP is unused. Any contradictory or ambiguous signal aborts deployment.

---

## 6. Software stack

### 6.1 Gateway

Use **LiteLLM Proxy** as the provider-normalization and virtual-key gateway.

Reasons:

- OpenAI-compatible API surface
- virtual keys
- authentication/authorization hooks
- spend tracking and budgets
- rate limiting
- provider normalization
- retries/fallbacks
- local OpenAI-compatible/vLLM endpoints
- OpenRouter and future external provider adapters

The custom Startup Teams control application remains authoritative for infrastructure-specific logic such as vLLM lifecycle, GPU health, power, benchmark qualification, host maintenance, and MARION-specific routing eligibility.

### 6.2 Custom control application

Recommended:

```text
Python 3.12
FastAPI
Uvicorn/Gunicorn
SQLAlchemy
Alembic
PostgreSQL
systemd
Nginx or Caddy
```

Suggested layout:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/
    app/
    control/
    collectors/
    routing/
    benchmark/
    auth/
    power/
    providers/
    distributed/
    migrations/
    static/
    templates/

/etc/llm-manager/
    manager.yaml
    nodes.yaml
    models.yaml
    routes.yaml
    tls/
    secrets/

/var/lib/llm-manager/
    cache/
    benchmark-artifacts/
    exports/
    spool/

/var/log/llm-manager/
```

### 6.3 Service separation

Use separate systemd units where practical:

```text
llm-manager-web.service
llm-manager-collector.service
llm-manager-benchmark.service
llm-manager-control.service
litellm.service
```

Do not let a hung benchmark worker take down the gateway.

---

## 7. Network, DNS, TLS, and access

Expose one HTTPS name:

```text
https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/
```

Recommended routing:

```text
/admin      -> custom admin UI
/api        -> manager orchestration API
/v1         -> LiteLLM/OpenAI-compatible API gateway
/healthz    -> manager health
```

Access policy:

```text
LAN:             allowed
Tailscale:       allowed where policy permits
Public Internet: denied
```

Hermes clients should only require:

```bash
OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1
OPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>
OPENAI_MODEL=startupteams/general
```

They should never require:

- SSH access to inference servers
- Proxmox credentials
- vLLM host credentials
- OpenRouter master credentials
- direct provider master credentials

---

## 8. Admin authentication and authorization

The web UI should authenticate against the existing LDAP/LLDAP environment.

Recommended roles:

```text
LLM-Viewer
LLM-Operator
LLM-Admin
LLM-Security-Admin
```

Example permissions:

| Capability | Viewer | Operator | Admin | Security Admin |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| View status/metrics | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Run benchmark | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Drain/undrain | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart vLLM service | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart inference VM | No | Restricted | Yes | Yes |
| Edit deployment config | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Create/rotate agent keys | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Change provider master secrets | No | No | No | Yes |
| Change global spend caps | No | No | Yes | Yes |

A local bootstrap/break-glass administrator may exist for installation/recovery, but LDAP should be the routine administrative identity source.

**Secret handling:** do not hardcode or reproduce the root password, LDAP bind password, Emporia password, OpenRouter key, or LiteLLM master key in this plan, source control, shell history, or generated configuration. Inject them through root-readable secret files or a later secret manager.

---

## 9. Hermes API-key and policy model

### 9.1 One key per Hermes agent

Structure:

```text
Organization
  -> Project/service
      -> Hermes agent
          -> one or more rotating API credentials
```

Each stored key record should include:

```text
key_id
key_prefix
hash
agent_id
project_id
created_at
expires_at optional
enabled
allowed_logical_models
max_concurrency
requests_per_minute optional
tokens_per_minute optional
daily_token_budget optional
monthly_token_budget optional
daily_dollar_budget optional
monthly_dollar_budget optional
local_allowed
cloud_allowed
fallback_policy
provider_allowlist optional
logging_policy
```

Raw keys are shown only at creation/rotation.

### 9.2 Initial policy profiles

Support at least:

```text
LOCAL_ONLY
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
CLOUD_ALLOWED_EXPLICIT
SPECIFIC_MODEL_ONLY
COST_CAPPED
BENCHMARK_ONLY
```

Recommended default for normal Hermes agents:

```text
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
```

but only after OpenRouter global caps are configured.

### 9.3 OpenRouter budget controls

Before enabling fallback for any default/general key:

```text
[ ] global daily spend cap configured
[ ] global monthly spend cap configured
[ ] warning threshold configured
[ ] hard-stop threshold configured
[ ] per-project cap supported
[ ] per-agent cap supported
[ ] audit event generated on fallback
[ ] actual OpenRouter reported cost stored when available
```

Fallback should never silently convert an uncapped local workload into uncapped external spend.

---

## 10. Routing and admission policy

### 10.1 Default sequence

For a normal general-purpose Hermes request:

```text
1. Resolve logical model and agent policy.
2. Find healthy local deployments that satisfy context/capability constraints.
3. Apply deployment admission limits.
4. If local capacity is temporarily full, queue for the configured local patience interval.
5. If admitted locally, let the local request continue even if TTFT is slow.
6. Do not start a duplicate OpenRouter request merely because local TTFT is slower than expected.
7. If no local deployment becomes admissible before the queue deadline, and the key permits cloud fallback, use OpenRouter.
8. If local execution actually fails after dispatch and a retry is safe, apply the configured retry/fallback policy.
9. If cloud fallback is disallowed or budget-capped, return an explicit unavailable/capacity response.
```

### 10.2 Initial timeout model

Use separate timeouts for separate failure modes.

Recommended starting configuration, to be benchmark-tuned:

```yaml
routing:
  local_queue_wait_seconds: 120
  local_connection_timeout_seconds: 15
  local_first_token_timeout_seconds: 300
  local_total_request_timeout_seconds: 1200
  cloud_fallback_on_queue_timeout: true
  cloud_fallback_on_slow_ttft_alone: false
  cloud_fallback_on_connection_failure: true
  cloud_fallback_on_local_5xx: policy_controlled
```

The values are intentionally generous because the objective is to use local inference when it is functioning, not optimize for minimum latency at any cost.

Per-model values should replace these defaults once sufficient history exists.

### 10.3 Capacity-aware admission

Every deployment stores a qualification envelope:

```text
qualified_max_context_per_request
qualified_concurrency
qualified_aggregate_context
recommended_concurrency
hard_concurrency_limit
KV cache dtype
GPU memory utilization target
preemptio

*(truncated, 52331 chars total)*

{"content": "1|#!/usr/bin/env python3\n2|\"\"\"Reliable password-SSH runner for MIAM nodes (pexpect, drain-hardened).\n3|\n4|Fixes the empty-output failure mode seen with ssh_run.py this session:\n5|sends the password, then waits for a SHELL PROMPT (miam-XXXXX#) before\n6|feeding the script, and drains until a literal END-MARKER echo.\n7|\"\"\"\n8|import base64\n9|import re\n10|import sys\n11|import time\n12|import pexpect\n13|\n14|END = \"___RUN_DONE_42___\"\n15|\n16|\n17|def run(host: str, script: str, user: str = \"root\", timeout: int = 120) -> str:\n18|    pw = open(\"/home/jordatech/.miam_root_pass\").read().strip()\n19|    b64 = base64.b64encode(script.encode()).decode()\n20|    wrapped = f\"echo {b64} | base64 -d | bash; echo {END}\"\n21|    child = pexpect.spawn(\n22|        \"/usr/bin/ssh\",\n23|        [\"-o\", \"StrictHostKeyChecking=no\", \"-o\", \"UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null\",\n24|         \"-o\", \"ConnectTimeout=10\", \"-l\", user, host, wrapped],\n25|        encoding=\"utf-8\", timeout=timeout, maxread=65536)\n26|    pieces = []\n27|    sent = False\n28|    deadline = time.time() + timeout\n29|    while time.time() < deadline:\n30|        i = child.expect([r\"[Pp]assword\\s*:\", END, pexpect.EOF, pexpect.TIMEOUT],\n31|                         timeout=15)\n32|        if i == 0 and not sent:\n33|            child.sendline(pw)\n34|            sent = True\n35|        elif i == 0 and sent:\n36|            # a second password prompt after sending = auth failure\n37|            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n38|            break\n39|        elif i == 1:\n40|            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n41|            break\n42|        elif i == 2:\n43|            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n44|            break\n45|        else:\n46|            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n47|    child.close()\n48|    text = \"\".join(pieces)\n49|    # strip \\r artifacts\n50|    text = text.replace(\"\\r\\n\", \"\\n\").replace(\"\\r\", \"\\n\")\n51|    if \"Permission denied\" in text:\n52|        text += \"\\n*** AUTH FAILED ***\"\n53|    return text\n54|\n55|\n56|if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n57|    host = sys.argv[1]\n58|    path = sys.argv[2]\n59|    script = open(path).read()\n60|    print(run(host, script))\n61|", "total_lines": 60, "file_size": 1966, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

### [2026-09-10T01:33:36.904667+00:00] tool · tool: terminal

{"output": "# OPNsense DNS bring-up via scripted web/API access (unbound host overrides)\n\nSession-verified recipe (2026-09) for creating DNS records on a MARION-lab OPNsense\nfirewall when no SSH access or API-key auth is available. All steps use plain Python\nstdlib (urllib + http.cookiejar) — no curl CSRF gymnastics required.\n\n## Topology facts (verify per environment, but expect this shape)\n\n- **Web UI / REST API: `http://10.0.10.1`** — plain HTTP, NOT HTTPS (TLS :443 refused).\n  The LAN-side DNS address `10.0.20.1` serves :53 only; it is NOT the web UI address.\n- SSH :22 closed; basic-auth REST login (`-u root:<UI-password>`) returns **401** —\n  OPNsense REST API requires generated API keys (System→Access→API), not the web login.\n  The path that works: UI session login + session-authenticated API calls (below).\n- Firewall hostnames seen: dashboard title `OPNsense.home.arpa`. Interfaces: `lan`\n  (LANJetKVM), `opt1` (2p5GSwitch), `opt2` (ISOLATED30), `wan` (WAN).\n\n## Auth chain (3 traps, in order)\n\n1. **UI login POST** (`/index.php`) needs FOUR things: the hidden randomized-CSRF field\n   (field NAME is random per load, e.g. `ktqFJ3L9NBFOo-...` — parse all\n   `<input type=\"hidden\">` tags and replay them), `usernamefld`, `passwordfld`, and the\n   **submit button field `login=1`**. Omitting `login:1` makes OPNsense silently re-render\n   the login page (len ~2.8 KB) — the classic silent-fail symptom.\n2. **Success check:** follow-up GET of `/index.php` returns the Dashboard (title\n   `Dashboard | Lobby | OPNsense.home.arpa`, len >100 KB). Session cookie auto-persists\n   via the cookie jar.\n3. **MVC API writes need the X-CSRFToken header.** It is NOT in a meta tag or hidden\n   input — it's embedded in the page's JS:\n   `xhr.setRequestHeader(\"X-CSRFToken\", \"<token>\")` in the `$.ajaxSetup` block of any\n   `/ui/...` page (fetch `/ui/unbound/general` or `/ui/unbound/overrides` and regex it\n   out: `r'setRequestHeader\\(\"X-CSRFToken\",\\s*\"([^\"]+)\"'`).\n\n## Unbound pages moved to MVC (classic .php pages 404)\n\n`/services_unbound.php` and `/services_unbound_host_overrides.php` are gone; the menu\nhrefs are `/ui/unbound/general`, `/ui/unbound/overrides`, etc. The backing REST endpoints\n( discoverable via `grep -oE \"/api/unbound/[a-z/]*\" <overrides page html>` ):\n\n- `GET  /api/unbound/settings/get` — full config model\n- `POST /api/unbound/settings/set` — save (see delta rule below)\n- `GET  /api/unbound/settings/searchHostOverride` — list host overrides\n- `POST /api/unbound/settings/addHostOverride` — body `{\"host\":{hostname,domain,rrtype:\"A\",server,description}}` → returns `{\"result\":\"saved\",\"uuid\":...}`\n- `POST /api/unbound/service/reconfigure` — body `{}` → `{\"status\":\"ok\"}`; this ACTIVATES saved changes\n\nAll of these work with just the session cookie + `X-CSRFToken` header after UI login.\n\n## Payload shape rules (500 \"Unexpected error\" avoidance)\n\n- **Post minimal deltas.** `{\"unbound\":{\"general\":{\"enabled\":\"1\"}}}` saves; posting the\n  full round-tripped GET object, partial objects with interface sub-dicts, boolean maps,\n  or `active_interface` as arrays all 500 (model applies `set` field-wise; unknown/extra\n  shapes are fatal, not ignored).\n- Multi-select fields in the GET model look like `{\"opt1\":{\"value\":\"2p5GSwitch\",\"selected\":0},...}`\n  or `[{\"value\":\"Level 1\",\"selected\":1},...]`. Do NOT try to reproduce that shape in\n  writes — empty/omitted multiselects mean \"all (recommended)\".\n- To listen on ALL internal interfaces (default, usually right), just enabling is enough.\n\n## The gotcha that cost hours: unbound installed but DISABLED\n\nSymptoms: port 53 times out from every LAN vantage point; everyone silently falls back to\npublic resolvers (CT/VM resolv.conf pointing at 1.1.1.1/8.8.8.8); override config exists\nbut never answers. Diagnosis: `POST /api/core/service/search` → service list shows no\n`unbound` running (kea-dhcp, pf, etc. present). Check\n`GET /api/unbound/settings/get` → `unbound.general.enabled` was `\"0\"`. Fix:\n`POST /api/unbound/settings/set {\"unbound\":{\"general\":{\"enabled\":\"1\"}}}` + reconfigure.\n\n## Client-side follow-through\n\nLXCs/VMs that hardcode public resolvers (PVE default template writes\n`nameserver 1.1.1.1`) bypass the firewall DNS entirely — override records won't resolve\nthere. For systemd-resolved guests (Ubuntu):\n```\n/etc/systemd/resolved.conf.d/firewall.conf:\n[Resolve]\nDNS=10.0.20.1\nDomains=<internal domain>\n```\nthen `systemctl restart systemd-resolved`, verify `getent hosts <name>`.\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n- `nslookup <name> 10.0.20.1` from a CT whose own resolv.conf points at 1.1.1.1 can time\n  out if egress/ACLs block UDP :53 cross-vlan — test DNS reachability from multiple\n  vantage points (management CT, the target VM, a node) before blaming the override.\n- The `.internal` TLD is deliberately not resolvable upstream — only unbound serves it;\n  every client that matters must use 10.0.20.1 as its resolver.\n- Don't store the firewall password in scripts — read from the profile credential file at\n  runtime and regex it out locally (same pattern as `scripts/ssh_run.py`).", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "Why we have different sizes for cudagraph and inductor:\n      - cudagraph: a cudagraph captured for a specific size can only be used\n          for the same size. We need to capture all the sizes we want to use.\n      - inductor: a graph compiled by inductor for a general shape can be used\n          for different sizes. Inductor can also compile for specific sizes,\n          where it can have more information to optimize the graph with fully\n          static shapes. However, we find the general shape compilation is\n          sufficient for most cases. It might be beneficial to compile for\n          certain small batchsizes, where inductor is good at optimizing.\n      \n  KernelConfig            Configuration for kernel selection and warmup behavior.\n  VllmConfig              Dataclass which contains all vllm-related configuration. {"content": "30|    try:\n31|        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=300) as r:\n32|            d = json.load(r)\n33|        ct = time.time()\n34|        c = d[\"choices\"][0]\n35|        ttft = d.get(\"usage\", {}).get(\"prompt_tokens\", 0)  # proxy\n36|        gen = d.get(\"usage\", {}).get(\"completion_tokens\", 0)\n37|        with lock:\n38|            results.append((idx, ttft, gen, ct - t0, c.get(\"finish_reason\")))\n39|    except Exception as e:\n40|        with lock:\n41|            errors.append((idx, repr(e)))\n42|\n43|def main():\n44|    workers = []\n45|    t_start = time.time()\n46|    for i in range(NUM_PROMPTS):\n47|        while sum(1 for w in workers if w.is_alive()) >= CONCURRENCY:\n48|            time.sleep(0.05)\n49|        w = threading.Thread(target=do_one, args=(i,))\n50|        w.start()\n51|        workers.append(w)\n52|    for w in workers:\n53|        w.join()\n54|    dt = time.time() - t_start\n55|    ok = [r for r in results]\n56|    ok_reqs = len(ok)\n57|    fail = len(errors)\n58|    total_in = sum(r[1] for r in ok)\n59|    total_out = sum(r[2] for r in ok)\n60|    print(f\"=== BENCH COMPLETE ===\")\n61|    print(f\"time: {dt:.1f}s  requests_ok: {ok_reqs}/{NUM_PROMPTS}  failed: {fail}\")\n62|    print(f\"total tokens in: {total_in}  out: {total_out}\")\n63|    print(f\"request throughput: {ok_reqs/dt:.2f} req/s\")\n64|    print(f\"output throughput:  {total_out/dt:.1f} tok/s\")\n65|    print(f\"total throughput:   {(total_in+total_out)/dt:.1f} tok/s\")\n66|    if errors[:3]:\n67|        print(\"sample errors:\", errors[:3])\n68|\n69|if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n70|    sys.stdout.flush()\n71|    main()", "total_lines": 70, "file_size": 2575, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"content": "1|#!/usr/bin/env python3\n2|\"\"\"Phase 8 benchmark engine v1 — LLM Manager.\n3|\n4|Presets per plan §18.1/§18.2. Runs against a target (default .161 direct), collects\n5|TTFT (real, via streaming first chunk), throughput, GPU power correlation, and\n6|stores results in PostgreSQL (llmmanager.benchmark_runs + benchmark_samples).\n7|\n8|Usage: bench_engine.py <preset> [--deployment http://10.0.20.161:8000] [--store]\n9|Presets: C1 C10 C16 C20 C32 C48 (4096-in/256-out, thinking-off)\n10|         required-qwen (identical to C10, the plan §18.2 comparison workload)\n11|\"\"\"\n12|import json, time, threading, urllib.request, statistics, sys, argparse, datetime\n13|import psycopg2\n14|\n15|\n16|def run_one(base_url, model, pad, out_len):\n17|    t0 = time.time()\n18|    payload = {\n19|        \"model\": model,\n20|        \"messages\": [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": pad + \" Summarize the key tradeoffs in one short paragraph.\"}],\n21|        \"temperature\": 0,\n22|        \"max_tokens\": out_len,\n23|        \"stream\": True,\n24|        \"chat_template_kwargs\": {\"enable_thinking\": False},\n25|    }\n26|    req = urllib.request.Request(base_url, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),\n27|                                 headers={\"Content-Type\": \"application/json\"})\n28|    ttft = None\n29|    out_tokens = 0\n30|    try:\n31|        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=600) as r:\n32|            for line in r:\n33|                if line.startswith(b\"data: \") and b\"[DONE]\" not in line:\n34|                    if ttft is None:\n35|                        ttft = time.time() - t0\n36|                    try:\n37|                        chunk = json.loads(line[6:])\n38|                        delta = chunk.get(\"choices\", [{}])[0].get(\"delta\", {})\n39|                        if delta.get(\"content\"):\n40|                            out_tokens += 1\n41|                    except Exception:\n42|                        pass\n43|        lat = time.time() - t0\n44|        RESULTS.append((ttft, lat, out_tokens))\n45|    except Exception as e:\n46|        ERRORS.append(repr(e)[:150])\n47|\n48|def run_bench(preset):\n49|    conc_map = {\"C1\": 1, \"C10\": 10, \"C16\": 16, \"C20\": 20, \"C32\": 32, \"C48\": 48}\n50|    conc = conc_map[preset]\n51|", "total_lines": 104, "file_size": 4404, "truncated": true, "hint": "Use offset=51 to continue reading (showing 1-50 of 104 lines)", "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

### [2026-09-10T09:49:51.783173+00:00] user

1. Open router key (please use GLM 5.3 Flash by default ) "vm906_llm_manager_fallback"=  sk-***REDACTED***

2. The spend accounting for local models should be dependent. It's a function of the electricity cost - we can use a rolling monthly average of the costs of running the total server and different parts of the server. You can use the total cost of the server as the high-end estimated margin cost divided by the total number of tokens produced. You can use the specific devices that are actually powering the large language model (for instance, the power coming from the GPUs on the servers, which is the majority of the power consumption). That average power over time, and calculate estimated costs. You can also do a capture of the average power for the latest daily or weekly usage costs, based on GPU power numbers and output token estimates as well as input token estimates, you can get some power numbers, once-a-day averages, from the power distribution units but I would prefer that you get most of your power numbers from Emporia so that we leave the PDUs open to be controlled instead of constantly using network traffic to monitor power through the PDUs. We do not want to block the PDUs with network traffic. 

3. If you want to set a static IP, go ahead and set a static IP. I don't understand why this is a roadblock. Just make sure it's not being used by anyone else. For instance 199 is in the DHCP pool so that would be inappropriate to set a static IP to that. The DHCP pool is from 190 to 250 so pick something outside that range that's not being used currently, preferably lower numbers just log in to the openSSH 10.0.10.1 firewall and see what is currently leased and what's available. Also monitor the static mappings from other virtual machines that are not currently running and make sure you don't take those static mappings. 

4. Please continue and do all of the phases for this plan. There's no reason to stop right now. Continue working until you have finished the job. Do not stop and if you hit a roadblock, work on other things that are related tasks. Make sure that you continue to work. Don't stop.

# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Plan vs. Implementation Status
**Date:** 2026-09-09 · **Reference:** `MARION-IA-USA_LLM_Manager_Implementation_Plan_v0.2.1.md`
**Question answered here:** *Have we implemented every part of the original plan? What remains?*

**Short answer: No — we have completed the core control-plane (roughly Phases 0–4, 7, 9, 10 partially, plus two unplanned repairs), but the majority of the operational/telemetry/qualification workstream (Phases 5–6, 8, 11–16) is still open.**

---

## 1. Completed (verified working as of 2026-09-09)

### Phase 0 — Read-only preflight ✅ (done, informal)
- [x] Cluster/node/guest inventory (PDM API); VMID + IP conflict checks
- [x] VM107/900/CT configs inspected; storage identified (no formal report format, but PREFLIGHT_REPORT.md exists)
- [x] Network audit vs. asset sheet — **no IP conflicts**; DHCP pool confirmed 10.0.20.190–.250

### Phase 1 — Manager VM bootstrap ✅
- [x] VM114 `marion-ia-usa-llm-manager` on MIAM-00135: 8 vCPU / 16 GB / 64 GB, static **10.0.20.108**, OVMF/q35, guest agent, nginx, hardened bootstrap (no GDI-clone path used — fresh noble cloud image instead, which satisfies the plan's clone-hygiene intent more cleanly)
- [x] Machine identity, TLS-capable nginx, `ubuntu` sudo user + ed25519 access

### Phase 2 — Application shell + LDAP + audit ✅ (v0.3.0)
- [x] FastAPI app on :8001 behind nginx `/login`, `/admin`
- [x] LDAP/LLDAP login + sessions + role gating (roles LLM-Viewer/Operator/Admin/Security-Admin map to LLDAP groups `llm-manager-vm114-*`; bind user `svc-llm-manager-bind`)
- [x] Audit logging to PostgreSQL (`llmmanager` DB)
- [x] **Exceeded plan:** working Keys panel (issue/block/unblock/revoke) and Deployments panel (view/edit systemd unit, restart vLLM, fetch logs) via fixed allowlist control agents on all 4 inference guests — no arbitrary shell

### Phase 3 — PostgreSQL ✅ (with a location change)
- [x] CT115 `llm-postgres` on **MIAM-00147** @ **10.0.20.116** (plan preferred 147; done. Original .115 collided with bare-metal node MIAM-00115 — found and fixed)
- [x] DBs: `llmmanager` (app/audit) + `litellm` (gateway state). DSN-driven config on both app and gateway
- [x] Secrets rotated (LiteLLM master key + PG app password)

### Phase 4 — Four-host monitoring ✅ (partial depth)
- [x] All 4 vLLM hosts serving `qwen3.6-35b-a3b` under systemd (VM103/.161, VM401/.162, VM109/.163, VM111/.164)
- [x] Health surfaced through the Deployments panel + control agents (status/unit/logs)
- ⚠️ *Depth gap:* no continuous sampler yet (plan's health_samples/gpu_samples ingestion = Phase 5 work)

### Phase 7 — Controlled lifecycle ✅
- [x] Fixed-allowlist guest control script `/opt/llm-control/llm-control.sh` (status/start/stop/restart/get-unit etc.), VM114-authorized SSH key per guest, audited actions from the UI
- ⚠️ *Gap:* VM start/reboot (Proxmox-level, plan §12.1) not yet wired into the app; bounded auto-recovery state machine (§12.3) not implemented

### Phase 9 — LiteLLM local gateway ✅
- [x] LiteLLM Proxy on VM114:4000, nginx-proxied at `/v1`; logical model `startupteams/general` load-balancing all 4 local backends
- [x] Virtual keys (one per Hermes agent pattern), budgets proven (0-budget key → 429), revocation, spend/token accounting in PG
- [x] `OPENAI_BASE_URL=http://10.0.20.108/v1` pattern ready for Hermes clients

### Phase 10 — First Hermes pilot ◐ (mostly)
- [x] Pilot virtual key `hermes-pilot-vm910` minted and E2E-verified through the gateway
- [x] DNS: `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` → 10.0.20.108 (via OPNsense unbound host override)
- ⬜ **The actual pilot VM does not exist yet** (CT900 Hermes Golden Master clone — user directive #7)

### Unplanned but done (user-directed fixes)
- [x] MIAM-00112 rebuild: VM401 restored from snapshot after another agent destroyed it; systemd vLLM unit adapted for 6× RTX 5060 Ti (Mamba-cache ceiling → `--max-num-seqs 64`, ctx 32768); in rotation
- [x] Raw `vllm serve` processes on .163/.164 adopted into proper systemd units
- [x] OPNsense DNS configured (unbound was disabled cluster-wide; enabled + host override)
- [x] Full IP-collision audit vs. asset sheet + secret rotation

---

## 2. Remaining — by plan phase

### 🔴 High priority (needed for the system to be genuinely useful)

# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Implementation Plan v0.2.1

**Status:** Design decisions incorporated; electricity-cost composition corrected; ready for read-only infrastructure preflight before deployment scripting  
**Decision date:** 2026-09-08  
**Target Proxmox host:** `MIAM-00135`  
**Candidate VMID:** `108` — **must be proven unused before creation**  
**Candidate IP:** `10.0.20.108` — **must be proven unused before assignment**  
**DNS name:** `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`  
**Primary purpose:** Central control plane, OpenAI-compatible API gateway, Hermes-agent key authority, routing layer, benchmarking system, observability system, and cost/performance database for the MARION-IA-USA local LLM infrastructure.

---

## 1. Executive objective

Create a dedicated VM named:

```text
MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
```

on `MIAM-00135`, initially bootstrapped from the known-good GPU Driver Installer controller VM architecture but fully re-identified and stripped of copied GDI secrets/state.

The LLM Manager will become the stable point through which local Hermes agents and other authorized internal clients use LLM inference. It should:

- monitor and control vLLM deployments on `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00112`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- issue a distinct API key to each Hermes agent
- expose a stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint
- route requests to healthy local deployments first
- wait a deliberate, configurable interval for local capacity instead of prematurely buying cloud inference
- fall back to OpenRouter only when policy permits and local capacity/health criteria are not satisfied
- enforce OpenRouter spend caps and per-agent policy
- benchmark model/topology combinations and keep historical results
- collect GPU, vLLM, request, health, and power telemetry
- calculate energy cost and performance-per-dollar
- use GPU onboard telemetry as the primary dynamic local power signal
- ingest Emporia whole-circuit/PDU/mini-split data as an independent facility-level measurement source
- store operational metadata and history in PostgreSQL
- offload high-volume or full prompt/response history away from the constrained `MIAM-00135` system disk
- provide controlled vLLM service lifecycle actions and bounded VM recovery actions
- provide a future path for multi-node vLLM deployments using the 100 Gb interconnect between `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- eventually compare/rank local inference, OpenRouter, direct APIs, and other officially supported provider integrations by availability, quality, cost, performance, context, privacy, and policy

The LLM Manager is a **control plane and gateway**. It does not normally host model weights or perform GPU inference itself.

---

## 2. Decisions locked in v0.2

The following answers from Section 28 of v0.1 are now treated as design decisions.

| Item | v0.2 decision |
|---|---|
| VM identity | Candidate `VMID 108`, candidate `10.0.20.108`; fail closed unless both are proven unused |
| Gateway | LiteLLM Proxy plus a Startup Teams custom management/control web application |
| Client keys | One distinct API key per Hermes agent |
| Qwen 3.6 normal load | Design around about 10 concurrent agents/requests, with an initial hard concurrency envelope of about 20 pending benchmark qualification |
| Routing default | Local first, deliberate wait/queue, OpenRouter fallback only if the key policy allows it |
| Cloud policy | Default OpenRouter master/aggregate budget must have hard caps; individual keys may override fallback permissions within administrator limits |
| VM control | Manager may control both vLLM service lifecycle and bounded inference-VM recovery/restart |
| Power | GPU telemetry first; modeled CPU/RAM/platform overhead; Emporia used as independent whole-circuit/facility telemetry |
| Electricity planning rate | Rate 400 base energy (`$0.09992/kWh` Sep-May; `$0.13398/kWh` Jun-Aug) + year-round Emporia-observed variable fees/riders adder of `$0.05830/kWh`; effective planning rates `$0.15822/kWh` Sep-May and `$0.19228/kWh` Jun-Aug (displayed as 15.82¢ and 19.23¢) |
| Logging | Metadata-first initially; architecture must support full prompt/response logs later with off-host storage |
| Admin auth | Existing LDAP/LLDAP identity system; local break-glass/bootstrap admin only if needed |
| DNS | `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` |
| Database | PostgreSQL; application designed so PostgreSQL can reside on a future storage-oriented VM on `MIAM-00147` |
| Fallback timing | Local requests are given meaningful time to queue/run; slow local TTFT alone is not a reason to immediately duplicate the request in OpenRouter |
| Distributed inference | 100 Gb multi-node path is a first-class future capability but remains unroutable until independently qualified |

### Important interpretation: API keys versus concurrency

The number of issued API keys should **not** be limited to 20. API keys identify clients; concurrency consumes model capacity.

For the known `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` deployment, start with a deployment profile such as:

```yaml
recommended_concurrency: 10
initial_hard_concurrency_limit: 20
max_issued_agent_keys: unlimited_by_model_capacity
```

The concurrency values must be model/deployment specific. A different model, quantization, topology, context target, or GPU set may receive a different admission envelope.

---

## 3. Known inference baseline

### 3.1 MIAM-00111 qualified baseline

Known qualified environment:

```text
Host:             MIAM-00111
Proxmox:          VE 9.2.10
Inference VM:     VM103 jordatech-vllm-test
Guest:            Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS
Kernel:           6.8.0-139-generic
GPUs:             6 x RTX 3080 20 GB
Aggregate VRAM:   120 GB
NVIDIA driver:    R580 open, module 580.173.02
Python env:       /opt/vllm-venv
API port:         8000
Model:            QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
```

Working topology:

```text
TP1 / DP6 / EP6
```

Core flags:

```text
--tensor-parallel-size 1
--data-parallel-size 6
--enable-expert-parallel
--expert-placement-strategy linear
--quantization awq
--dtype half
--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92
--kv-cache-dtype turboquant_k8v4
--attention-backend TURBOQUANT
--enable-prefix-caching
--enable-chunked-prefill
--scheduler-reserve-full-isl
--watermark 0.05
--kv-cache-metrics
```

Validated large-context operating point:

```text
10 concurrent requests
229,376 input tokens per request
+ 256 output tokens per request
```

Do not record the synthetic 250k test as a qualified model limit because tokenizer expansion exceeded the declared 262,144-token model maximum.

Representative 4096-in/256-out throughput results:

| Concurrency | Output tok/s | Total tok/s | Mean TTFT |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 249.85 | 4259.49 | 4.27 s |
| 16 | 271.21 | 4623.65 | 4.73 s |
| 20 | 311.98 | 5318.69 | 5.20 s |
| 32 | 319.69 | 5450.14 | 7.39 s |
| 48 | 348.62 | 5943.41 | 11.13 s |

For normal Hermes service, concurrency 10 remains the initial preferred baseline even though aggregate throughput rises at higher concurrency.

### 3.2 MIAM-00112

Known major difference:

```text
6 x RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB
AMD Threadripper Pro 3955WX
128 GB RAM
```

Do not copy the MIAM-00111 topology without qualification. Its 96 GB aggregate VRAM and different GPU architecture require a separate model-fit and benchmark envelope.

### 3.3 MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144

These are intended to reproduce or complement the MIAM-00111 inference stack, but each must independently inventory:

```text
GPU model/count/VRAM
PCI BDFs
IOMMU groups
kernel
NVIDIA driver/module packages
inference VM resources
100 Gb NICs/ports
network addresses
RDMA capability
GPU-to-NIC PCIe topology
```

Never copy PCI BDFs between hosts.

---

## 4. High-level architecture

```text
       Hermes agents / internal clients / LXCs / VMs
                         |
                         | HTTPS
                         | per-agent LLM Manager key
                         v
       +-------------------------------------------+
       | MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager                 |
       | MIAM-00135 / candidate VM108              |
       | llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal        |
       +-------------------------------------------+
          |             |               |
          |             |               +------------------+
          |             |                                  |
          v             v                                  v
   LiteLLM Gateway   Custom Admin UI                Benchmark/collectors
   /v1/...           /admin + /api                  health/power/metrics
          |             |                                  |
          +-------------+--------------------+-------------+
                                             |
                                      PostgreSQL DSN
                                             |
                     +-----------------------+------------------------+
                     |                                                |
             bootstrap/local DB                              preferred long-term DB
             only if necessary                           dedicated VM on MIAM-00147

                         LOCAL ROUTING
          +----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+
          |                |                |                |
          v                v                v                v
      MIAM-00111       MIAM-00112       MIAM-00143       MIAM-00144
       vLLM VM          vLLM VM          vLLM VM          vLLM VM
          |                |                |                |
          +----------------+----------------+----------------+
                         |
                optional cloud fallback
                         v
                     OpenRouter
```

Separate telemetry path:

```text
GPU NVML/nvidia-smi ----+
vLLM /metrics ----------+----> LLM Manager collectors ----> PostgreSQL
OS/VM health -----------+
Emporia cloud ----------+----> Emporia adapter -----------> PostgreSQL
```

Experimental distributed inference path:

```text
MIAM-00111 <==== 100 Gb ====> MIAM-00143
     ^                           |
     |                           |
   100 Gb                      100 Gb
     |                           |
     +======== MIAM-00144 <======+

        multi-node vLLM / NCCL / RDMA qualification lane
```

`MIAM-00112` is not included in this ring unless a suitable high-speed connection is later documented and qualified.

---

## 5. Base VM and bootstrap strategy

### 5.1 Candidate VM sizing

Start with:

```text
VMID:             108 candidate only
IP:               10.0.20.108 candidate only
Hostname:         marion-ia-usa-llm-manager
Display name:     MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
OS:               Ubuntu Server 24.04 LTS
vCPU:             8
RAM:              16 GB
System disk:      64 GB
Local data disk:  64-128 GB only if actually available/needed
Machine:          q35
Firmware:         OVMF
QEMU agent:       enabled
Ballooning:       disabled
Autostart:        enabled after acceptance test
GPU passthrough:  none
```

Because `MIAM-00135` has constrained storage, do not assume a large local telemetry/database disk. Keep all high-volume paths configurable and relocatable.

### 5.2 Clone source

Use the existing controller:

```text
VM107
gpu-driver-installer-v4-1
10.0.20.107:8443
```

as a **bootstrap image only**.

Required clone hygiene:

1. Full-clone while source is in a consistent state.
2. Keep clone network-isolated until identity reset is complete.
3. Change VM display name and guest hostname.
4. Regenerate machine-id.
5. Regenerate SSH host keys.
6. Remove copied application secrets, private keys, audit records, state, tokens, and TLS identity.
7. Remove GDI application code/services not needed by LLM Manager.
8. Retain only useful base hardening, QEMU guest agent, service patterns, reverse-proxy/TLS patterns, and OS configuration.
9. Generate a new TLS certificate for `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`.
10. Run a secret scan before enabling the new network interface.

### 5.3 Fail-closed VMID/IP preflight

Before any clone or network mutation, the deployment script must prove both identifiers are unused.

VMID validation should include at least:

```text
cluster-wide Proxmox resource lookup for VMID 108
MIAM-00135 qm list
/etc/pve configuration search
```

IP validation should include at least:

```text
existing Proxmox guest configuration search
DNS lookup
neighbor/ARP table lookup
ICMP probe
ARP duplicate-address detection from the correct VLAN
DHCP/static reservation inspection where available
```

A failed probe does **not** prove an IP is unused. Any contradictory or ambiguous signal aborts deployment.

---

## 6. Software stack

### 6.1 Gateway

Use **LiteLLM Proxy** as the provider-normalization and virtual-key gateway.

Reasons:

- OpenAI-compatible API surface
- virtual keys
- authentication/authorization hooks
- spend tracking and budgets
- rate limiting
- provider normalization
- retries/fallbacks
- local OpenAI-compatible/vLLM endpoints
- OpenRouter and future external provider adapters

The custom Startup Teams control application remains authoritative for infrastructure-specific logic such as vLLM lifecycle, GPU health, power, benchmark qualification, host maintenance, and MARION-specific routing eligibility.

### 6.2 Custom control application

Recommended:

```text
Python 3.12
FastAPI
Uvicorn/Gunicorn
SQLAlchemy
Alembic
PostgreSQL
systemd
Nginx or Caddy
```

Suggested layout:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/
    app/
    control/
    collectors/
    routing/
    benchmark/
    auth/
    power/
    providers/
    distributed/
    migrations/
    static/
    templates/

/etc/llm-manager/
    manager.yaml
    nodes.yaml
    models.yaml
    routes.yaml
    tls/
    secrets/

/var/lib/llm-manager/
    cache/
    benchmark-artifacts/
    exports/
    spool/

/var/log/llm-manager/
```

### 6.3 Service separation

Use separate systemd units where practical:

```text
llm-manager-web.service
llm-manager-collector.service
llm-manager-benchmark.service
llm-manager-control.service
litellm.service
```

Do not let a hung benchmark worker take down the gateway.

---

## 7. Network, DNS, TLS, and access

Expose one HTTPS name:

```text
https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/
```

Recommended routing:

```text
/admin      -> custom admin UI
/api        -> manager orchestration API
/v1         -> LiteLLM/OpenAI-compatible API gateway
/healthz    -> manager health
```

Access policy:

```text
LAN:             allowed
Tailscale:       allowed where policy permits
Public Internet: denied
```

Hermes clients should only require:

```bash
OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1
OPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>
OPENAI_MODEL=startupteams/general
```

They should never require:

- SSH access to inference servers
- Proxmox credentials
- vLLM host credentials
- OpenRouter master credentials
- direct provider master credentials

---

## 8. Admin authentication and authorization

The web UI should authenticate against the existing LDAP/LLDAP environment.

Recommended roles:

```text
LLM-Viewer
LLM-Operator
LLM-Admin
LLM-Security-Admin
```

Example permissions:

| Capability | Viewer | Operator | Admin | Security Admin |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| View status/metrics | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Run benchmark | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Drain/undrain | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart vLLM service | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart inference VM | No | Restricted | Yes | Yes |
| Edit deployment config | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Create/rotate agent keys | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Change provider master secrets | No | No | No | Yes |
| Change global spend caps | No | No | Yes | Yes |

A local bootstrap/break-glass administrator may exist for installation/recovery, but LDAP should be the routine administrative identity source.

**Secret handling:** do not hardcode or reproduce the root password, LDAP bind password, Emporia password, OpenRouter key, or LiteLLM master key in this plan, source control, shell history, or generated configuration. Inject them through root-readable secret files or a later secret manager.

---

## 9. Hermes API-key and policy model

### 9.1 One key per Hermes agent

Structure:

```text
Organization
  -> Project/service
      -> Hermes agent
          -> one or more rotating API credentials
```

Each stored key record should include:

```text
key_id
key_prefix
hash
agent_id
project_id
created_at
expires_at optional
enabled
allowed_logical_models
max_concurrency
requests_per_minute optional
tokens_per_minute optional
daily_token_budget optional
monthly_token_budget optional
daily_dollar_budget optional
monthly_dollar_budget optional
local_allowed
cloud_allowed
fallback_policy
provider_allowlist optional
logging_policy
```

Raw keys are shown only at creation/rotation.

### 9.2 Initial policy profiles

Support at least:

```text
LOCAL_ONLY
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
CLOUD_ALLOWED_EXPLICIT
SPECIFIC_MODEL_ONLY
COST_CAPPED
BENCHMARK_ONLY
```

Recommended default for normal Hermes agents:

```text
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
```

but only after OpenRouter global caps are configured.

### 9.3 OpenRouter budget controls

Before enabling fallback for any default/general key:

```text
[ ] global daily spend cap configured
[ ] global monthly spend cap configured
[ ] warning threshold configured
[ ] hard-stop threshold configured
[ ] per-project cap supported
[ ] per-agent cap supported
[ ] audit event generated on fallback
[ ] actual OpenRouter reported cost stored when available
```

Fallback should never silently convert an uncapped local workload into uncapped external spend.

---

## 10. Routing and admission policy

### 10.1 Default sequence

For a normal general-purpose Hermes request:

```text
1. Resolve logical model and agent policy.
2. Find healthy local deployments that satisfy context/capability constraints.
3. Apply deployment admission limits.
4. If local capacity is temporarily full, queue for the configured local patience interval.
5. If admitted locally, let the local request continue even if TTFT is slow.
6. Do not start a duplicate OpenRouter request merely because local TTFT is slower than expected.
7. If no local deployment becomes admissible before the queue deadline, and the key permits cloud fallback, use OpenRouter.
8. If local execution actually fails after dispatch and a retry is safe, apply the configured retry/fallback policy.
9. If cloud fallback is disallowed or budget-capped, return an explicit unavailable/capacity response.
```

### 10.2 Initial timeout model

Use separate timeouts for separate failure modes.

Recommended starting configuration, to be benchmark-tuned:

```yaml
routing:
  local_queue_wait_seconds: 120
  local_connection_timeout_seconds: 15
  local_first_token_timeout_seconds: 300
  local_total_request_timeout_seconds: 1200
  cloud_fallback_on_queue_timeout: true
  cloud_fallback_on_slow_ttft_alone: false
  cloud_fallback_on_connection_failure: true
  cloud_fallback_on_local_5xx: policy_controlled
```

The values are intentionally generous because the objective is to use local inference when it is functioning, not optimize for minimum latency at any cost.

Per-model values should replace these defaults once sufficient history exists.

### 10.3 Capacity-aware admission

Every deployment stores a qualification envelope:

```text
qualified_max_context_per_request
qualified_concurrency
qualified_aggregate_context
recommended_concurrency
hard_concurrency_limit
KV cache dtype
GPU memory utilization target
preemptio

*(truncated, 52331 chars total)*
